\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{n}[\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}(s)\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{r-\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil},S}(s)+\gamma\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil},S}(s^{\prime})\}]+(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil},S}(s_{0})],

\displaystyle\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}

\displaystyle=\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{S1}}\max_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{n}[w(s)\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{-v(s)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]+(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v(s_{0})]-\lambda\|\mathcal{J}v\|^{2}_{2,n},

\displaystyle\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil},S}

\displaystyle=\min_{v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}_{2}}\max_{w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{S2}}\mathbb{E}_{n}[w(s)\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{r-v(s)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]-\lambda^{\prime}\|w\|^{2}_{2,n},

where
\mathcal{J}v=-v(s)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})
. This recovers as special cases the estimators of
(
Liu
et al. 2018
;
Feng
et al. 2019
;
Tang
et al. 2020
)
when we do not use stabilizers (
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}=0
). Even for these special cases, our theoretical guarantees are novel. For simplicity, we assume each function class
\mathbb{W}_{S1},\mathbb{W}_{S2},\mathbb{V}_{1},\mathbb{V}_{2}
is symmetric. And, for the rest of the subsection, assume
\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)
is known (
i.e.
,
\pi^{\mathrm{b}}
is known).

We define the state-based Bellman and transition operators, respectively, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{S}:~f\mapsto\left(s\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s),r\sim p(r|s,a),s^{\prime}\sim p(s^{\prime}|s,a)}[r+\gamma f(s^{\prime})]\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{S}:~f\mapsto\left(s\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s),r\sim p(r|s,a),s^{\prime}\sim p(s^{\prime}|s,a)}[f(s^{\prime})]\right).

We again define
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}
as the adjoint operator of
\mathcal{T}_{S}
s.t.
\langle f,\mathcal{T}_{S}g\rangle=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}f,g\rangle
on
L_{2}(S)
.
And, we define the state-based backward Bellman operator as

\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{S}:~f\mapsto\left(s\mapsto\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f(s)+(1-\gamma)\frac{d_{0}(s)}{P_{S}(s)}\right).

Finally, we let
\mathcal{T}_{\gamma,S}=\gamma\mathcal{T}_{S}-I
,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma,S}=\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}-I.

First we adapt
Theorem

4.1
to state-based MIL estimators under
w_{S}
- and
v
-reazalibility.

Theorem 8.1

(Finite sample error bound of state-based MIL estimators under realizability )
.

Set
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}=0
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{V})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{w(s)\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)(-v(s)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})):w\in\mathbb{W}_{S},\,v\in\mathbb{V}\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{S}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W}_{S1},\mathbb{V}_{1}))+C_{\mathbb{W}_{S1}}(R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{V}_{1}})\sqrt{\log(c_{0}/\delta)n^{-1}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{S}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}_{S1})+R_{\max}C_{\mathbb{W}_{S1}}\sqrt{\log(c_{0}/\delta)n^{-1}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},v}

\displaystyle\coloneqq R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}_{S2})+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W}_{S2},\mathbb{V}_{2}))+C_{\mathbb{W}_{S2}}(R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{V}_{2}})\sqrt{\log(c_{0}/\delta)n^{-1}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},wv}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W}_{S1},\mathbb{V}_{2}))+C_{\mathbb{W}_{S1}}(R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{V}_{2}})\sqrt{\log(c_{0}/\delta)n^{-1}}.

1.

If
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\in\mathbb{W}_{S1},v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{V}_{1}
, then
|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w,S}-J|\lesssim_{\delta}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{S}}+\mathrm{Err}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{S}}
.

2.

If
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\in\mathbb{W}_{S2},v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
, then
|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{v}-J|\lesssim_{\delta}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},v}
.

3.

If either
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\in\mathbb{W}_{S1},v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-\mathbb{V}_{2}\subset\mathbb{V}_{1}
or
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}-\mathbb{W}_{S1}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S2},v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{V}_{2}
, then
|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wv}-J|\lesssim_{\delta}\max(\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{S}},\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},v})+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},wv}
.

Comparing to
Theorem

4.1
, the error bounds of state-based MIL methods are generally tighter since the complexities of
\mathbb{W}_{S}
and
\mathbb{V}
should be smaller than the ones of
\mathbb{W}
and
\mathbb{Q}
, as the former are marginalizations of the latter and have smaller input dimension. The caveat is that the knowledge of
\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)
plays a critical role in
Theorem

8.1
. When
\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)
is unknown (even if it is estimated and plugged in),
Theorem

8.1
no longer holds.

Next, we consider the adaptation of
Theorem

5.1
to state-based MIL estimators under realizability and completeness of
w_{S}
- or
v
-functions.

Theorem 8.2

(Convergence rates in state-based MIL estimators under completeness and realizability )
.

Set
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}=0
. For a problem-dependent constant
C_{\xi}
,

1.

If
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\in\mathbb{W}_{S1},C_{\xi}(\mathbb{W}_{S1}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S})\subset\mathcal{T}_{\gamma,\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{V}_{1}
then
\|\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\delta}\sqrt{C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{\mathcal{S}}}}
,

2.

If
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S}\in\mathbb{W}_{S1},C_{\xi}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma,\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{W}_{S1}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},S})\subset\mathbb{V}_{1}
then
\|\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}}\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\delta}\sqrt{C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w_{\mathcal{S}}}}
,

3.

If

v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{V}_{2},C_{\xi}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma,\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{V}_{2}-v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{W}_{S2}
then
\|\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil}}-\mathcal{B}_{\mathcal{S}}\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\delta}\sqrt{C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},v}}
,

4.

If

v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{V}_{2},C_{\xi}(\mathbb{V}_{2}-v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma,\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{W}_{S2}
, then
\|\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil}}-v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\delta}\sqrt{C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},v}}
.

For example,
Item

2
in
Theorem

8.2
says when we assume
w_{S}
-realizability and
w_{S}
-completeness, we have a convergence guarantee for
\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}
. By a state-based analog of Lemma
2.2
,
w_{S}
-completeness condition is also equivalent to
C_{\xi}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{S}-I)\mathbb{W}_{S1}\subset\mathbb{V}_{1}
.
Similarly, the
v
-completeness condition (
Item

3
in
Theorem

8.2
) is equivalent to
C_{\xi}(\mathcal{B}_{S}-I)\mathbb{V}_{2}\subset\mathbb{W}_{S2}
.

The aforementioned results are slow rates. As in
Section

6
, when we use stabilizers
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}>0
, we can obtain
fast rates
for
\|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{S}\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}-\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil},S}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathcal{B}_{S}\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil}}-\widehat{v}_{\mathrm{mil}}\|_{2}
by analogously modifying
Theorems

6.1
and
6.2
to stated-based estimators as done in the above theorems.
We omit the details for the sake of brevity.
We note that by utilizing these fast-rate results along with sample splitting, an extension of
Theorem

7.1
would yield that, under
w_{S}
-relizability,
w_{S}
-completeness ,
v
-realizability,
v
-completeness , recovery assumption, and sufficiently fast rates to eliminate higher order terms,
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wv,S}
has a finite sample guarantee of the form

\displaystyle\sqrt{{2\mathbb{E}[w^{2}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{r-v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s)+\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}^{2}]\log(1/\delta)}/{n}}+o(1/\sqrt{n}).

(11)

This, however, is
not
first-order efficient since the leading constant is generally
larger
than
Eq.

10
(see also Remark 7 in
(
Kallus and
Uehara 2019
)
). In this specific sense, state-based methods can be inefficient.
The leading constant does agree, however, with the asymptotic efficiency bound in
(
Kallus and
Uehara 2020b
)
for evaluating natural stochastic policies, where state-based method
are
efficient.

8.2
Policy Optimization

In (off-policy) policy optimization we seek to use off-policy data to find a new, well-performing policy. Our results can be directly extended to this task in two different ways.

The first way is a policy-based way
(
Athey and
Wager 2017
;
Kallus 2018
)
. In this case, given a policy class
\Pi
and an OPE estimator
\widehat{J}(\pi)
for the policy value of any one policy
\pi
, we can pick a policy by
\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\widehat{J}(\pi)
. We can, for example, use MIL for
\widehat{J}(\pi)
.
When
\Pi
is finite, we can simply take union bound over our results in order to immediately obtain a regret bound for this policy optimization method. When
\Pi
is infinite we need to take care to chain our results over successive approximations thereof, given for example a cover entropy condition on
\Pi
. We leave the details of this to future work.

An alternative to this is the value-based approach, where we seek to estimate
q^{*}
, the (true)
q
-function of the (true) optimal policy, and then deploy the policy that is greedy with respect to our estimate.
Define the Bellman
optimality
operator as

\mathcal{B}^{*}:~f\to\left((s,a)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}[r+\gamma\max_{a}f(s^{\prime},a)]\right),

and note that
q^{*}
satisfies the fixed point equation
q^{*}=\mathcal{B}^{*}q^{*}
.
This, in turn, implies the following set of moment conditions:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[w(s,a)\{r-q^{*}(s,a)+\gamma\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}q^{*}(s^{\prime},a)\}],\quad\forall w(s,a).

Similar to the MIL estimator
\widehat{q}_{\mathrm{mil}}
, motivated by the above moment condition, we can consider the following estimator for
q^{*}
and the corresponding learned policy:

\displaystyle\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}=\argmin_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{n}[w(s,a)\{r-q(s,a)+\gamma\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}q(s^{\prime},a)\}]-\lambda^{\prime}\|w\|^{2}_{2,n},

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}(\widehat{a}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}(s)|s)=1,~~\text{for any choice}~~\widehat{a}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}(s)\in\argmax_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}(s,a).

When
\lambda^{\prime}=0
, this is equivalent to MABO
(
Xie and
Jiang 2020
)
and when
\lambda^{\prime}=0.5
to MBRM
(
Antos
et al. 2008
)
.

We can extend our results of MIL to the above value-based policy optimization method. First, we consider the case where
\lambda^{\prime}=0
.
Let
\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}=\{s\mapsto\argmax_{a}q(s,a);q\in\mathbb{Q}\}
be the policy class induced by
\mathbb{Q}
.

Theorem 8.3
.

Set
\lambda^{\prime}=0
.
Suppose
1
holds for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}
. If
w_{\pi}\in\mathbb{W}
for
\forall\pi\in\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
q^{*}\in\mathbb{Q}
, then

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}}J(\pi)-J(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}})\lesssim(1-\gamma)^{-1}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{ope}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{ope}}\coloneqq\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G})+\sqrt{\frac{C_{\mathbb{W}}(R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{Q}})\log(1/\delta)}{n}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\{w(s,a)\{r-q(s,a)+\gamma\max_{a}q(s,a)\};w\in\mathbb{W},q\in\mathbb{Q}\}.

This is analogous to the second statement in
Theorem

4.1
. Note that assuming
1
holds for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}
subsumes the standard concentrability condition for policy optimization,
\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\|w_{\pi}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{w}

(
Munos and
Szepesvári 2008
)
. The rate of
\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G})
can calculated exactrly as in
Section

4.1
. Note
(
Xie and
Jiang 2020
)
bounds the regret of MABO (
\lambda^{\prime}=0
) only for a finite hypothesis class.

Next, we consider the case when
\lambda^{\prime}>0
, extending
Theorem

6.2
to optimization.

Theorem 8.4
.

Let
\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}
be an upper bound on the critical radii of
\mathbb{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{G}_{q}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{q}:=\{(s,a,s^{\prime})\mapsto(-q(s,a)+q^{*}(s,a)+\gamma[\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}q(s^{\prime},a)-\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}q^{*}(s^{\prime},a)])w(s,a):q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}\}.

Suppose
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}
,
C_{\xi}(\mathcal{B}^{*}-I)\mathbb{Q}_{2}\subset\mathbb{W}_{2}
, and that
\mathbb{W}_{2}
is star shaped. Then,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}^{*}\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}-\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\delta}C^{*}_{q,n}\eta_{q,n},

\displaystyle C^{*}_{q,n}=1+R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}}+\left(\lambda^{\prime 2}+(R_{\max}+C_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}})^{2}\right)/{\lambda^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{C_{\xi}},

\displaystyle\eta_{q,n}=\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}+c_{0}\sqrt{{\log(c_{1}/\delta)}/{n}}.

Corollarry 8.5
.

Under the conditions in
Theorem

8.4
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\mathbb{Q}}}J(\pi)-J(\widehat{\pi}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}})\leq\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\|w_{\pi}\|_{2}C^{*}_{q,n}\eta_{q,n}.

The fast convergence rate for
\|\mathcal{B}^{*}\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}-\widehat{q}^{*}_{\mathrm{mil}}\|_{2}
is, to the best our knowledge, novel even for MBRM (
\lambda^{\prime}=0.5
). The rate of
\eta_{q,n}
for each model can calculated exactly as in
Section

6.2
.

9
Discussion of Related Work

FQI

FQI is a method to estimate
q
-functions by iterating regression so that the one-step Bellman residual error is minimized
(
Ernst
et al. 2005
;
Munos and
Szepesvári 2008
;
Chen and
Jiang 2019
;
Fan
et al. 2020
)
. FQI gives the same rate as
\widehat{q}_{\mathrm{mil}}
with stabilizers wrt
n
under
q
-completeness and -realizability. Since most of the work on FQI focuses on policy optimization (Bellman optimality residual error), in
Appendix

E
we modify the existing FQI analysis for policy evaluation for a direct comparison.

Modified BRM and DICE

Modified BRM (Bellman Residual Minimization;

(
Antos
et al. 2008
)
) is equivalent to
\widehat{q}^{\mathrm{mil}}
with
\lambda=0.5
.
(
Farahm et al. 2016
)
obtained a fast rate for modified RBM when
\mathbb{W}_{2},\mathbb{Q}_{2}
are nonparametric classes by directly assuming smoothness of the transition operators instead of completeness. Comparing to
(
Farahm et al. 2016
)
, our analysis can handle more general function classes such as neural networks. In addition, the analysis is substantially different since the assumptions are different. Similar comparisons applies to
(
Liao
et al. 2021
;
Liao
et al. 2020
)
, which discuss the fast rate of modified RBM in the average-reward setting. A series of papers consider several minimax RL estimators with stabilizers under the generic name DICE
(
Zhang
et al. 2020
;
Nachum
et al. 2019
)
. They typically obtain a rate
O(1/\sqrt{n})
for the policy value assuming the function classes have finite VC dimensions. In our work, we show how the rate changes depending on the various assumptions on
\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}
. To our knowledge, they do not discuss our main results:
w
-completeness, fast rates for
q
- and
w
-estimation, and first-order efficiency.

Finally,
(
Duan
et al. 2021
)
, whose preprint was posted shortly after ours, also obtained a fast-rate result for modified BRM with general function approximation for policy optimization in a time-varying MDP, which corresponds to our result in
Section

8.2
. Comparing to
(
Duan
et al. 2021
)
, our setting is a time-homogeneous MDP (a.k.a infinite horizon setting). In addition, they do not discuss our main results:
w
-completeness, fast rates for
w
-estimation, and first-order efficiency.

Tabular and linear cases

(
Yin
et al. 2020
;
Yin and
Wang 2020
)
analyzed the finite sample error bound of model-based estimators in a tabular time-varying MDP and showed that the the first-order lower bound is achieved. Our result is much more general since it allows general function approximation, covers both the non-tabular and tabular cases, and applies to the more common time-homogeneous case.
(
Duan
et al. 2020
;
Hao et al. 2020
;
Wang
et al. 2020
)
considered linear models and showed upper and lower bounds, but their results are specialized to the linear function approximation setting.

Non-dynamic (bandit) setting

In the non-dynamic setting (
\gamma=0
, also termed the logged bandit problem or cross-sectional causal inference), similar results to
Theorem

7.1
are obtained by
(
Foster and
Syrgkanis 2019
;
Smucler
et al. 2019
;
Chernozhukov
et al. 2018
;
Narita
et al. 2019
;
Benkeser
et al. 2017
)
. The RL setting is much more complicated in that we need to take dynamics and therefore completeness into account. The proof idea of
Theorems

6.2
and
6.1
is inspired by
(
Dikkala
et al. 2020
)
, which studies the minimax estimation of nonparametric instrumental variable problems. The adaption to our setting is non-trivial since the problem for estimating
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is not characterized by a conditional moment equation.

Efficiency bounds in RL

Efficiency bounds are commonly used in causal inference to discuss the optimality of estimators
(
Robins
et al. 1994
;
Tsiatis 2006
;
Kosorok 2008
)
. The efficiency bound in our setting (time-homogeneous MDPs) is derived by
(
Kallus and
Uehara 2019
)
, which in turn was extended to the average reward setting by
(
Liao
et al. 2020
)
and the non-stationary time-homogeneous MDP setting by
(
Bibaut et al. 2021
)
. In all of these papers, the final results for OPE estimators are primarily asymptotic and a polynomial dependence on problem dependent quantities such as horizon is not shown. We focus on the more challenging non-asymptotic analysis. Our result can also be extended to the closely related time-varying MDP setting. In this setting, the efficiency bound in the tabular case is derived by
(
Jiang and Li 2016
)
and in the general case by
(
Kallus and
Uehara 2020a
)
.

Dynamic treatment regimes

OPE is also often studied in the context of dynamic treatment regimes
(
Kosorok and
Moodie 2015
;
Tsiatis 2020
)
. The data generating process in this context is typically non-Markovian and time-inhomogeneous, which we call an NMDP. Though NMDPs are very general, methods for OPE in NMDPs have errors with exponential dependence on horizon
(
Thomas and
Brunskill 2016
;
Jiang and Li 2016
;
Zhang
et al. 2013b
;
Tsiatis 2006
)
. In fact, the efficiency bounds under NMDP is exponential in horizon so this is unavoidable
(
Kallus and
Uehara 2020a
)
. In contrast, in MDPs, by leveraging the Markovian property, one can avoid exponential dependence, such as we have obtained here.

10
Summary and Future Work

We analyzed the rates of MIL estimators under four cases: (1)
w
- and
q
-realizability, (2)
w
-realizability and -completeness, (3)
q
-realizability and -completeness, (4)
w
- and
q
-realizability and
w
- and
q
-completeness.
While our generic results for MIL with general function approximation are new in each setting,
settings (2) and (4) are particularly novel, showing OPE is feasible in new cases not studied previously. And, in (4), together with a recovery assumption, we establish the first finite-sample bound with first-order efficiency in general settings. We further extended our results to state-based estimation and to policy optimization.

A few important questions remain. The first is a lower bound under each assumption. The first-order efficiency bound is an asymptotic notion and only concerns the leading term in
n
. Crucially, our finite-sample bounds also establish full polynomial dependence on horizon and other parameters, not just in the
1/\sqrt{n}
term. Corresponding non-asymptotic lower bounds for each setting, showing whether the dependence in all terms is optimal, is therefore interesting future work. Recent work
(
Wang
et al. 2020
;
Amortila
et al. 2020
)
has shown exponential-in-horizon lower bounds with only
q
-realizability. Our work shows that adding
either

w
-realizability or
q
-completeness yields polynomial upper bounds.

Another important direction is to further explore policy optimization. We briefly discuss in
Section

8.2
how to adapt our OPE results to policy optimization in a relatively straightforward way. Recent work empirically shows that some penalization is crucial to deal with the inevitable issue of weak overalp
(
Yu et al. 2020
;
Kidambi et al. 2020
)
. Theoretically, such penalization may help avoid dependence on the worst-case overlap over the policy class,
\sup_{\pi\in\Pi}\|w_{\pi}\|_{\infty}

(
Rashidinejad et al. 2021
)
. How to incorporate this idea into the MIL framework is an interesting question.

Appendix B
Adjoint Transition Operators

B.1
Specific form of adjoint operators

Lemma B.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:f(s,a)\mapsto\eta(s,a)\int\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S}(s)}\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}).

When
P_{S}(s)=P_{S^{\prime}}(s)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:f(s,a)\mapsto\eta(s,a)\int P_{S,A|S^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}|s)f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}).

Proof.

The first statement is proved by

\displaystyle\textstyle\langle f,\mathcal{T}g\rangle

\displaystyle=\textstyle\int f(s,a)\{\int g(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\}P_{S,A}(s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a)

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int f(s,a)g(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a)P_{S,A}(s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a,s^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})g(s,a)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a,s^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int g(s,a)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a,s^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int g(s,a)\frac{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}P_{S,A}(s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a,s^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int g(s,a)\frac{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})p_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{b}}(a|s)}P_{S,A}(s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a,s^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int g(s,a)\eta(s,a)\{\int\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S}(s)}\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\}P_{S,A}(s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s,a)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f,g\rangle.

Here, we use the assumption
P_{S^{\prime}}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)/P_{S,A}(s,a)\leq C_{m}C_{\eta}
for
\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{X}
in
1
.

Next, we use the assumption
P_{S}(s)=P_{S^{\prime}}(s)
. Then, the second statement is proved by

\displaystyle\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S}(s)}=\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S^{\prime}}(s)}

\displaystyle=P_{S,A|S^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}|s).

∎

B.2
Examples

When we restrict
L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
to more restricted finite dimensional classes, the operators
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
are represented by matrices. We define
a\cdot b=a^{\top}b
.

Example 11

(Tabular model)
.

We use a matrix formulation. Let
\mathcal{X}:=S\times A
, and also
\mathcal{P}^{\pi}
be an
|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|\times|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|
matrix, where its
\mathcal{P}^{\pi}_{s^{\prime}a^{\prime},sa}\coloneqq\Pr(s_{t+1}=s^{\prime},a_{t+1}=a^{\prime}|s_{t}=s,a_{t}=a,a_{t+1}\sim\pi(\cdot|s_{t+1}))
. Given a function
f(s,a)
on
\mathcal{X}
, we represents this
f(s,a)
as a
|S||A|\times 1
vector and denote it by
[f]:=(f(x_{1}),\cdots,f(x_{|S||A|}))^{\top}
. In a tabular case, the feature vector
\phi(s,a)
is given as
(I((s,a)=x_{1}),\cdots,I((s,a)=x_{|S||A|}))^{\top}
. Thus,
f(s,a)=[f]^{\top}\phi
. Note we often take

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2}=\mathbb{W}_{2}=\mathbb{Q}_{1}=\mathbb{W}_{1}=\{(s,a)\mapsto\theta^{\top}\phi(s,a);\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{|S||A|}\}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}f]=P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}[f],\,[\{(\gamma\mathcal{T}-I)f\}]=(\gamma P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)[f],

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\phi=(P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})^{\top}\phi,\,(\gamma\mathcal{T}-I)\phi=(\gamma P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)^{\top}\phi.

In addition,
\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]
is a diagonal matrix whose
(i,i)
-th entry corresponding
x_{i}\in\mathcal{X}
, is
\mathbb{E}[I((s,a)=x_{i})]
, i.e.,
P_{S,A}(x_{i})
. This is non-singular when
P_{S,A}(s,a)>0\,\forall(s,a)\in\mathcal{X}
. Then,
\langle f,g\rangle=\mathbb{E}[fg]
is

\displaystyle\langle f,g\rangle=[f]^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}][g].

In addition, from

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}f,g\rangle=\{P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}[f]\}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}][g]=[f]^{\top}\{P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}][g]=[f]^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}\{P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}[g],

and
\langle\mathcal{T}f,g\rangle=\langle f,\mathcal{T}^{\prime}g\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f]=(\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}(P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}])[f],\,[(\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-I)f]=(\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}(\gamma P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}])[f],

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\phi=(\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}(P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}])^{\top}\phi=\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]P^{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}\phi.

Example 12

(Linear models -ver 1-)
.

Consider a
d
-dimensional feature vector
\phi
. For
f_{1}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{1}\phi,f_{2}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{2}\phi
, the inner product is defined as

\displaystyle\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=\theta^{\top}_{1}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}.

Then, suppose that we have a matrix s.t.

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{T}\phi=M^{\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\phi,\,\mathcal{T}(\theta\cdot\phi)=M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\theta\cdot\phi.

This matrix
M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is referred to as a matrix mean embedding of the conditional transition operator
(
Duan
et al. 2020
)
. In linear MDPs,
M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is explicitly calculated. See Lemma
5.2
. We consider an adjoint operator of this operator. For
f_{1}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{1}\phi,f_{2}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{2}\phi
, then,

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=(M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\theta_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}=\theta^{\top}_{1}M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}=\theta^{\top}_{1}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}

when
\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]
is non-singular. From
\langle\mathcal{T}f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=\langle f_{1},\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f_{2}\rangle
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:f_{2}\to\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}\cdot\phi.

(12)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\phi

\displaystyle=\{\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{\top}\phi

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}\phi\

Example 13

(Linear models -ver 2-)
.

Consider a
d
-dimensional feature vector
\phi
. For
f_{1}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{1}\phi,f_{2}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{2}\phi
, the inner product is defined as

\displaystyle\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=\theta^{\top}_{1}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}.

Then, suppose that we have a matrix s.t.

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\phi=M^{\prime\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\phi,\,\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(\theta\cdot\phi)=M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\theta\cdot\phi

In the case of Lemma
5.4
,
M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is explicitly calculated. See Lemma
5.4
. We consider an adjoint operator of this operator. For
f_{1}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{1}\phi,f_{2}(s,a)=\theta^{\top}_{2}\phi
, then,

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=(M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\theta_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}=\theta^{\top}_{1}{M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}=\theta^{\top}_{1}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}{M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}

when
\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]
is non-singular. From
\langle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=\langle f_{1},\mathcal{T}f_{2}\rangle
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}:f_{2}\to\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}{M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\theta_{2}\cdot\phi.

(13)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\phi

\displaystyle=\{\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}{M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{\top}\phi

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]{M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}}\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}\phi.

Appendix C
Theorems Agnostic to Realizability and Completeness

We present the theorems agnostic to realizability and completeness . These theorems are useful to analyze sieve estimators and neural networks when
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{W}
grow with
n
.

First, we discuss the extension of
Theorem

4.1
, which takes the bias terms into account. We only present the result of
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mdl}}_{w1}
and
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q},\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w},\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}
.

Theorem C.1
.

When
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}=0
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}-J|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},q}+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})],

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w}+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})],

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}-J|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},wq}+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w^{\prime}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})],

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}-J|

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},wq}+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q^{\prime}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})].

In
Theorem

C.1
, the term
\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]
represents the discrepancy from the assumption
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}
. The term
\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
represents the discrepancy from the assumption
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{W}_{2}
. A similar observation is made in
Theorem

C.1
for the other estimators.

Next, we present the extension of
Theorem

6.2
, which takes the bias terms into account. We can similarly extend
Theorem

6.1
.

Theorem C.2
.

Let
\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}
be an upper bound on the critical radius of
\mathbb{W}
and
\mathcal{G}_{q}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{q}:=\{(s,a,s^{\prime})\mapsto\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-\gamma q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\}w(s,a);q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}\}.

Assume
\mathbb{W}_{2}
is symmetric and star-shaped. By defining
\epsilon_{n}\coloneqq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\|w-\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{2}
, for any
q_{0}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}
, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{2}\lesssim(1+C_{1}+\lambda+C^{2}_{1}/\lambda)\eta_{q,n}+\epsilon_{n}+\frac{\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-q_{0})\|^{2}_{2}}{\lambda\eta_{q,n}}+\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-q_{0})\|_{2},

where
\eta_{q,n}=\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}+c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log(c_{1}/\delta)}{n}}
for universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}
.

When
q_{0}=q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
and
\epsilon_{n}=0
, this result is reduced to
Theorem

6.2
. In
Theorem

C.2
,
\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-q_{0})\|_{2}
represents the discrepancy from the condition
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}
. The term
\epsilon_{n}
represents the discrepancy from the condition
\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{2}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{W}_{2}
.

Appendix D
Fast Rates for
L^{2}
-errors

In the main text, we show how to derive the fast rates in terms of Bellman residual errors. We show how to obtain the fast rates in terms of
L^{2}
-errors.

Theorem D.1
.

Let
\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}
be an upper bound on the critical radius of
\mathbb{W}_{2}
and
\mathcal{G}_{q}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{q}:=\{(s,a,s^{\prime})\mapsto\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-\gamma q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\}w(s,a);q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}\}.

Assume
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},C_{\xi}(\mathbb{Q}_{2}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathbb{W}_{2},\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}\|w\|/\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}w\|\leq C_{\iota}
,
\mathbb{W}_{2}
is symmetric and star-shaped. Then, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{q}_{\mathrm{mil}}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\lesssim(1+C_{1}+C_{1}^{2}/\lambda^{\prime}+C_{\mathbb{W}_{2}}(1+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})+1/C_{\xi}+\lambda^{\prime}\{1+C^{2}_{\iota}\})\eta_{q,n}\,

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=C_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}}+(1-\gamma)^{-1}R_{\max},

where
\eta_{q,n}=\eta^{\prime}_{q,n}+c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log(c_{1}/\delta)}{n}}
for universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}
.

Theorem D.2
.

Let
\eta^{\prime}_{w,n}
be an upper bound on the critical radius of
\mathcal{G}_{w1},\mathcal{G}_{w2}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{w1}:

\displaystyle=\{(s,a,s^{\prime})\mapsto\{q(s,a)-\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\};q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{w2}:

\displaystyle=\{(s,a,s^{\prime})\mapsto\{w(s,a)-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\}\{-q(s,a)+\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\};w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}\}.

Suppose
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{W}_{1}
,
C_{\xi}(\mathbb{W}_{1}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\mathbb{Q}_{1},\,\sup_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}\|q\|/\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q\|\leq C_{\iota}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{1}
is symmetric and star-shaped. Then, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{w}_{\mathrm{mil}}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\lesssim\left\{1+C^{2}_{\mathbb{W}_{1}}/\lambda+C_{\mathbb{W}_{1}}+C_{\mathbb{Q}_{1}}+1/C_{\xi}+\lambda\{1+C^{2}_{\iota}(1+C_{m}C_{\eta})\}\right\}\eta_{w,n}

where
\eta_{w,n}=\eta^{\prime}_{w,n}+c_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log(c_{1}/\delta)}{n}}
for universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}
.

Appendix E
Complete Analysis of FQI for Policy Evaluation

The FQI has been analyzed in detail
(
Munos and
Szepesvári 2008
;
Chen and
Jiang 2019
)
for policy optimization.
For policy evaluation,
(
Le
et al. 2019b
)
analyzes FQI in detail when the hypothesis classes are VC-classes. For the sake of completeness, we show the analysis when using general function classes. To make the analysis easier, we consider the sample splitting version of FQI:

1.

Set the number of iterations
T
, and split the whole
n
sample into
T
bins. We denote the
t
th bin by
\zeta_{t}(t\in[1,\cdots,T])
.

2.

At the initial stage
t=0
, take some function
f_{0}
.

3.

Repeat the following from
t=1,\cdots,T

\displaystyle f_{t}=\argmin_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\sum_{j\in\zeta_{t}}\{f_{t-1}(s^{\prime j},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})+r^{j}-q(s^{j},a^{j})\}^{2}.

4.

Define the estimator
\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[f_{T}(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})]
.

Theorem E.1
.

We set the critical radius of
\mathrm{star}(\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
as
\eta_{t}
at
t
-th stage, i.e., a solution to

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\zeta_{t}}(\eta;\mathbb{Q})\leq\eta^{2}/C_{\mathbb{Q}}.

where
\mathcal{R}_{\zeta_{t}}(\eta;\mathbb{Q})
is an empirical localized Rademacher complexity of
\mathbb{Q}
based on the data
\zeta_{t}
. Suppose
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q},\mathcal{B}\mathbb{Q}\subset\mathbb{Q}
. With probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d_{0}}[f_{T}(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}})]-J|\lesssim\frac{(1-\gamma^{T/2})}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\|w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}C_{\mathbb{Q}}\eta_{q}+\gamma^{{T}/{2}}C_{\mathbb{Q}},

where
\eta_{q}=\max_{t\in[1,\cdots,T]}(\eta_{t}+\sqrt{c_{0}\log(c_{1}T/\delta)/n_{t}})
for some universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}
.

Proof.

We directly combine the two lemmas, Lemma
E.2
and Lemma
E.3
. The statement is immediately concluded.
∎

The critical radius
\eta_{q}
is similarly calculated as in
Section

6
. For example, when
\mathbb{Q}
belongs to VC classes, and the VC dimension is
V(\mathbb{Q})
, then,

\displaystyle\eta_{q}=O(\max_{t\in[1,\cdots,T]}(\sqrt{V(\mathbb{Q})/n_{t}}+\sqrt{c_{0}\log(c_{1}T/\delta)/n_{t}})).

The above theorem concludes that the error of FQI is roughly
\eta_{q}
when we only focus on the sample size. This result is equivalent to the analysis of
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}
with stabilizers as in Corollary
6.7
. Note that the dependence of
\|w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}
is the same.

Lemma E.2
.

We set the critical radius of
\mathrm{star}(\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
as
\eta_{t}
at
t
-th stage, i.e., a solution to

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\zeta_{t}}(\eta;\mathbb{Q})\leq\eta^{2}/C_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Suppose
\mathcal{B}\mathbb{Q}\subset\mathbb{Q}
. Then, with probability
1-\delta
,
\|f_{t}-\mathcal{B}f_{t-1}\|_{2}\lesssim C_{\mathbb{Q}}\eta

where
\eta=\eta_{t}+\sqrt{c_{0}\log(c_{1}/\delta)/n_{t}}
for some universal constants
c_{0},c_{1}
.

Lemma E.3
.

If we have
\|f_{t}-\mathcal{B}f_{t-1}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon
for any
T\geq t\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d_{0}}[(1-\gamma)f_{T}(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}})]-J]|\leq\frac{\{1-\gamma^{T/2}\}(1+\gamma^{1/2})}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\|w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\varepsilon+2\gamma^{{T}/{2}}(1-\gamma)C_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Appendix F
Proofs

F.1
Proof of
Section

2

Proof of Lemma
2.1
.

We show the operator
\mathcal{T}
is bounded:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}\|=\sup_{u\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\|\mathcal{T}u\|_{2}/\|u\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}.

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}u\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a),a^{\prime}\sim\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})}[u(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})]\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a),a^{\prime}\sim\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime}),(s,a)\sim P_{S,A}}[u(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})^{2}]

(Jensen)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim P_{S,A}}\left[\frac{P_{S^{\prime}}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)}{P_{S}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{b}}(a|s)}u(s,a)^{2}\right]\leq C_{m}C_{\eta}\|u\|^{2}_{2}.

Them, the adjoint operator is uniquely defined following standard theory of Hilbert spaces
(
Debnath 2005
, Theorem 4.3.13)
. In addition, note that the adjoint operator is also bounded:
\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\|=\|\mathcal{T}\|\leq\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

Statement
(\mathcal{B}-I)q=\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}-I)q

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a),P_{R|S,A}(r|s,a)}[r+\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-q(s,a)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a)}[\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-\gamma q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)]

\displaystyle=(\gamma\mathcal{T}-I)(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})=\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}).

Statement
\mathcal{B}q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}

This is proved by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}.

Statement
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}-I)w=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})

We check

\displaystyle\textstyle-\frac{(1-\gamma)d_{0}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}=(\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-I)w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}.

This is proved as

\displaystyle\textstyle(\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-I)\frac{d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s,a)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\gamma\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\int\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S}(s)}\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})-\frac{d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s,a)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\gamma\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)\int\frac{P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})-\frac{d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s,a)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}

\displaystyle=\textstyle-\frac{(1-\gamma)d_{0}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}.

We use Lemma 11
(
Uehara
et al. 2020
)
:

\displaystyle\gamma\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)\int P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s|s^{\prime},a^{\prime})d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})-d_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}},\gamma}(s,a)=-(1-\gamma)d_{0}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s).

Thus, We have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}-I)w

\displaystyle=\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w+\frac{(1-\gamma)d_{0}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)}{P_{S,A}(s,a)}-w

\displaystyle=(\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-I)(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}).

Statement
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}

This is proved by

\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})+w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.3
.

Statement:
\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}
and
\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}
are equivalent

We prove

\displaystyle(1-\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathcal{B}q-q\|_{2}\leq(1+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

First,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}q-q\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(\gamma\mathcal{T}-I)(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\|\mathcal{T}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{2}+\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}+\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

(From the proof of Lemma
2.1
)

In addition,

\displaystyle\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{B}q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}=\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{B}q-\mathcal{B}q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}+\gamma\|\mathcal{T}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

This shows
\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\{1-\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}\}\leq\|q-\mathcal{B}q\|_{2}.

Statement:
\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}
and
\|w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w\|_{2}
are equivalent

We prove

\displaystyle(1-\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w-w\|_{2}\leq(1+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

First,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w-w\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-I)(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w-\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}+\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

(From the proof of Lemma
2.1
)

Besides,

\displaystyle\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w\|_{2}+\|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w\|_{2}+\gamma\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w-\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w\|_{2}+\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}}\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}.

Thus,
(1-\gamma\sqrt{C_{m}C_{\eta}})\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}\leq\|w-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w\|_{2}
.

∎

F.2
Proof of
Section

4

Proof of
Theorem

4.1
.

Proof for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}

Estimators for the weight functions in MIL are defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{w}=\argmin_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]|,

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)=w(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\}-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[q(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})].

We write this solution to the above minimax optimization as
(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\widehat{q}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\argmax_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};\widehat{w},q)]|.

We define
J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)r]
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}\{\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}]|.

The following is the proof. We have for any
q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}
:

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)r]-J|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v(s_{0})]|

\displaystyle+|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-v(s_{0})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v(s_{0})]||

\displaystyle+|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}(s,a)-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\}\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]|.

Then, by defining

\displaystyle q^{\diamond}=\argmin_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}(s,a)-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\}\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]|.

we have

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{q^{\diamond}(s,a)-\gamma v^{\diamond}(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v^{\diamond}(s_{0})]|

(14)

\displaystyle+|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}(s,a)-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\}\{-q^{\diamond}(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma v^{\diamond}(s^{\prime})-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]|

(15)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v(s_{0})]|

\displaystyle+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}(s,a)-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\}\{-q(s,a)+q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})-\gamma v_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime})\}]|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}\{\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}]|.

(16)

Here, we use
\mathbb{E}[w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}]-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[v(s_{0})]=0
from
(
Uehara
et al. 2020
)
. Since the second term is zero under
q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}
, we further analyze the first term:

\displaystyle\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|.

Recall we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w,q)]=\mathbb{E}[-(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q].

Then, using
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}
, from
Eq.

16
,

\displaystyle|J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|=|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}=\argmax_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|.

As a first step, we bound
|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|
:

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w,q)]|.

Here, we use
|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|\leq|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|
and
|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|=0
.

Second, we calculate the difference of
|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|
and
|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|
.

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|-|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w,q)]|.

Here, we use
|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widetilde{q})]|\leq|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(\widehat{w},\widehat{q}^{\dagger})]|
. Thus,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|-|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|+|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widehat{q}^{\dagger}]|

\displaystyle\leq 4|\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w,q)]|.

In addition, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}|\leq\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}|(\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E})[w(s,a)r]|.

Combining all results together and using the uniform law of large numbers, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{w}-J|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}|(\mathbb{E}_{n}-\mathbb{E})[w(s,a)r]|+4|\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(w,q)]|

\displaystyle\lesssim R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}_{1})+R_{\max}\sqrt{\log(4/\delta)/n}+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W}_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{1}))+C_{\mathbb{W}_{1}}(C_{\mathbb{Q}_{1}}+R_{\max})\sqrt{\log(4/\delta)/n}.

Proof for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}

Estimators for q-functions in MIL are defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{q}=\argmin_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]|,\,f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)=w(s,a)\{r-q(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}

We write this solution as
(\widehat{q},\widehat{w}^{\dagger})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{w}^{\dagger}=\argmax_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,\widehat{q})]|.

We have

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}-J|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w(s,a)\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

(17)

This is proved as follows. We have for any
w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}
:

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}-J|

\displaystyle=|(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[\widehat{q}(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})]-\mathbb{E}[w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)r]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{-\widehat{q}(s,a)+\gamma\widehat{v}(s^{\prime})\}]-\mathbb{E}[w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)r]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\{r-\widehat{q}(s,a)+\gamma\widehat{v}(s^{\prime})\}]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[w(s,a)\{r-\widehat{q}(s,a)+\gamma\widehat{v}(s^{\prime})\}]|+|\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\{r-\widehat{q}(s,a)+\gamma\widehat{v}(s^{\prime})\}]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|+|\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

This concludes the proof of
Eq.

17
.

Then, recall

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]=\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})].

Then, from the assumption and (
17
), we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}-J|\leq\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w(s,a)\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|=|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}=\argmax_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

As a first step, we bound
|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
:

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(q,w)]|

Here, we use
|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|\leq|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
and
|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|=0
.

Second, we bound the difference of
|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
and
|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
:

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|-|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|

\displaystyle\quad+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|+|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|-|\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(q,w)]|.

Here, we use
|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widetilde{w},\widehat{q})]|\leq|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(\widehat{w}^{\dagger},\widehat{q})]|
. In the end,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|-|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|+|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{w}^{\dagger}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|

\displaystyle\leq 4|\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(q,w)]|.

This term is upper-bounded by
\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W}_{2},\mathbb{Q}_{2}))+C_{\mathbb{W}_{2}}C_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\sqrt{\log(2/\delta)/n}
up to universal constants.

Proof for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}

Letting

\displaystyle f(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)=w(s,a)\{r-q(s,a)+\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\}+(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[q(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})],

we define

\displaystyle J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}=\mathbb{E}[f(s,a,r,s^{\prime};\widehat{w},\widehat{q})].

Here, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}-J^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}|\leq\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f(w,q)]|.

Besides,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(s,a,r,s^{\prime};\widehat{w},\widehat{q})]-J=|\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}\{\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}]|.

Case 1: when
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2},w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-\mathbb{W}_{1}\subset\mathbb{W}_{2}

Thus, from a similar calculation as the above,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[f(s,a,r,s^{\prime};\widehat{w},\widehat{q})]-J|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w(s,a)\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[\{w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}+\widehat{w}\}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

The second term is
0
from the assumption. The first term is calculated as before.

Case 2: when
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-\mathbb{Q}_{2}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{1}

Thus, from a similar calculation as the above,

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[f(s,a,r,s^{\prime};\widehat{w},\widehat{q})]-J|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\{q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}+\widehat{q}\}]|.

The second term is
0
from the assumption. The first term is calculated as before.

Combining all things, with
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{wq}-J|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f(w,q)]|

\displaystyle+\max(4\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(q,w)]|,4\sup_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2},q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}|(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{n})[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(q,w)]|)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},wq}+\max(\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w},\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},q}).

∎

Proof of Corollary
4.2
.

First part

First, we prove that the VC dimension of
\mathbb{V}\coloneqq\{q(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}});q\in\mathbb{Q}\}
is upper-bounded by
V(\mathbb{Q})
. Consider the set of subgraphs of
\mathbb{V}
as
\mathcal{V}
. Then, we take
n
points
s_{1},\cdots,s_{n}
shattered by
\mathbb{V}
. This states for any binary sequence
\{b_{1},\cdots,b_{n}\}
, there exists
v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{V}
and
t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s_{1},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-t^{\prime}>0)=b_{1},\cdots,\mathrm{I}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s_{n},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-t^{\prime}>0)=b_{n},

where
q_{v^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Q}
is a corresponding function such as
v^{\prime}(s)=q_{v^{\prime}}(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}})
.
We, then, consider the set of subgraphs of
\mathbb{Q}
as
\mathcal{Q}
, and show that
\mathcal{Q}
can shatter
(s_{1},a_{1}),...,(s_{n},a_{n})
associated with
s_{1},...,s_{n}
.
To bound the VC dimension of
\mathbb{V}
, it is sufficient to show

\displaystyle\exists a,~\mathrm{sign}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}})-t^{\prime})=\mathrm{sign}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s,a)-t^{\prime}),

(18)

holds for any
s
.
To show (
18
), without loss of generality, we assume
q_{v^{\prime}}(s,\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\geq t^{\prime}
, which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\textstyle\int q_{v^{\prime}}(s,a)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)\mathrm{d}(a)>t^{\prime}.

(19)

We use the contradiction argument. Suppose there is no
a\in\mathcal{A}
s.t.
q_{v^{\prime}}(s,a)>t^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\textstyle\int q_{v^{\prime}}(s,a)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)\mathrm{d}(a)\leq t^{\prime},

holds.
This contradicts
Eq.

19
, hence there exists
a\in\mathcal{A}
s.t.
q_{v^{\prime}}(s,a)>t^{\prime}
.
Hence, we can take
\{a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}\}
as satisfying (
18
) for each
s_{1},...,s_{n}
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s_{1},a_{1})-t^{\prime}>0)=b_{1},\cdots,\mathrm{I}(q_{v^{\prime}}(s_{n},a_{n})-t^{\prime}>0)=b_{n}.

This concludes that
V(\mathbb{V})\leq V(\mathbb{Q})
.

Second part

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Q})+\gamma\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{V})

\displaystyle\leq 0.25\{\mathcal{R}_{n}((\mathbb{W}+\mathbb{Q})^{2}-(\mathbb{W}-\mathbb{Q})^{2})+\gamma\mathcal{R}_{n}((\mathbb{W}+\mathbb{V})^{2}-(\mathbb{W}-\mathbb{V})^{2})\}

\displaystyle\leq\{C_{\mathbb{W}}C_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}+\mathbb{Q}))+\gamma C_{\mathbb{W}}C_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}+\mathbb{V})\}

(Contraction property
(
Mendelson 2002
)
)

Here, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W})

\displaystyle\textstyle=\int_{0}^{C_{\mathbb{W}}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(t,\mathbb{W},\|\cdot\|_{n})/n}\mathrm{d}(t)

\displaystyle\textstyle=O\left\{\int_{0}^{C_{\mathbb{W}}}\sqrt{V(\mathbb{W})\log(1/t)/n}\mathrm{d}(t)\right\}

\displaystyle\textstyle=O(\sqrt{V(\mathbb{W})/n}).

The covering number is calculated using
(
van der
Vaart 1998
, Lemma 19.15)
, that is, for a VC class of functions
\mathcal{F}
with measurable envelope function
F
and
r\geq 1
, one has for any probability measure
Q
with
\|F\|_{Q,r}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(\epsilon\|F\|_{Q,r},\mathcal{F},L_{r}(Q))\lesssim V(\mathcal{F})(4e)^{V(\mathcal{F})}\left(\frac{2}{\epsilon}\right)^{rV(\mathcal{F})}.

Thus,

\displaystyle R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W})+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))=O(\sqrt{(V(\mathbb{W})+V(\mathbb{Q}))/n}).

This concludes that with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}-J|=O(\sqrt{(V(\mathbb{W})+V(\mathbb{Q}))/n}).

∎

Proof of Corollary
4.3
.

WLOG, we prove the case for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}
. From Lemma
F.6
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\tau,\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\log(0.5\tau/((1+\gamma)C_{\mathbb{Q}}),\mathbb{W},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})+\log\mathcal{N}(0.5\tau/((1+\gamma)C_{\mathbb{W}}),\mathbb{Q},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\log(0.5\tau/((1+\gamma)C_{\mathbb{Q}}),\mathbb{W},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}+\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(0.5\tau/((1+\gamma)C_{\mathbb{W}}),\mathbb{Q},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}.

First, we consider the case when
\beta<2
, from Lemma
F.4
, the final error is

\displaystyle O\left(n^{-1/2}\left\{1-\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\right\}^{0.5\beta-1}\right\}+\tau\right).

By taking
\tau=\Theta(1/n)
, the final error is
O(n^{-1/2})
.

Next, we consider the case when
\beta=2
. From Lemma
F.4
, the final error is

\displaystyle O\left(n^{-1/2}\{1-\log(\tau)\}+\tau\right).

By taking
\tau=\Theta(\log(n)/\sqrt{n})
, the final error is
O(n^{-1/2}\log n)
.

Finally, consider the case when
\beta\geq 2
. From Lemma
F.4
, the final error is

\displaystyle O\left(n^{-1/2}\left\{\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\right\}^{0.5\beta-1}-1\right\}+\tau\right).

Here, by taking
\tau=\Theta(n^{-1/\beta})
, the final error is
\widetilde{O}(n^{-1/\beta})
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.4
.

WLOS, we prove the case for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}
. From
Theorem

C.1
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}-J|

\displaystyle=O(R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W})+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q})))

\displaystyle+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]).

Then, from the assumption of sieve space
\mathbb{W}
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]

\displaystyle\lesssim\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\|w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{\infty}(C_{\mathbb{Q}}+R_{\max}(1-\gamma)^{-1})

(from
\|\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\|_{1}\lesssim C_{\mathbb{Q}}+R_{\max}(1-\gamma)^{-1})
)

\displaystyle=O(k^{-p/d}_{n}).

Similarly, from the assumption of sieve space
\mathbb{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]\leq(1+\gamma)C_{\mathbb{W}}\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\|q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{\infty}=O(k^{-p/d}_{n}).

In addition, we have
R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W})+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))=O(\sqrt{k_{n}/n})
from Corollary
4.2
. In the end, the final error becomes

O(\textstyle\sqrt{k_{n}/n}+k^{-p/d}_{n}).

Balancing these two terms, the final error is
O(n^{-\frac{p}{2p+d}})
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.5
.

WLOS, we prove the case for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}
.
As a similar discussion as the proof of Corollary
4.4
,
|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}-J|
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle O(R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}))+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]

(
Theorem

C.1
)

\displaystyle=O(R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}))+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))+\widetilde{O}(\Xi^{-p/d})

(Lemma
F.8
)

\displaystyle=O(\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{NN}))+\widetilde{O}(\Xi^{-p/d})

( Contraction property )

\displaystyle=O\left(\sqrt{\Xi L/n}\int_{0}^{C_{NN}}\sqrt{\log(LB(\Xi+1)/t)}\mathrm{d}\mu(t)\right)+\widetilde{O}(\Xi^{-p/d}),

(Lemma
F.7
and Dudley integral)

where
\mathcal{F}_{NN}\leq C_{NN}=\max\{C_{\mathbb{W}},C_{\mathbb{Q}}\}
. Then, by taking
L=\Theta(\log n),\Xi=\Theta(n^{d/(2p+d)})
, the statement is concluded.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.6
.

WLOS, we prove the case for
\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}_{q}
. We define
C_{NN}:=\max\{C_{\mathbb{W}},C_{\mathbb{Q}}\}
which is finite by the boundedness of
A_{\ell}
for
\mathcal{F}_{NN}
.
As a similar discussion as the proof of Corollary
4.4
,
|\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{mil}}-J|
is upper-bounded by

\displaystyle O(R_{\max}\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathbb{W}))+\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{W},\mathbb{Q}))+\min_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{E}[(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]+\min_{q\in\mathbb{Q}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}}\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]

(
Theorem

C.1
)

\displaystyle=O(\mathcal{R}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{NN})+\epsilon).

( Contraction property and the assumption for the approximation error)

We apply Lemma
F.9
and obtain the statement.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

Convergence rate of w-functions regarding
\|\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

Estimators for the weight functions in MIL are defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{w}=\argmin_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{1}}\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]|,

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)=w(s,a)\{q(s,a)-\gamma q(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\}-(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}_{d_{0}}[q(s_{0},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})].

We write this solution to the above minimax optimization as
\widehat{w}
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{1}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]=-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})q].

What we want to bound is
\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})^{2}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})^{2}]\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[C^{-1}_{\xi}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|=|\mathbb{E}[C^{-1}_{\xi}\{(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\widetilde{q}]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}=\argmax_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}q]|.

Here, we use
C_{\xi}(\mathbb{W}_{1}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}\mathbb{Q}_{1}
. Therefore, if we can bound
|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\widetilde{q}]|
, the convergence rate is obtained. This is bounded as in the proof of
Theorem

4.1
. Thus, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]\leq C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w}.

Convergence rate of w-functions regarding
\|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\widehat{w}-\widehat{w}\|_{2}

What we want to bound is
\mathbb{E}[(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\widehat{w}-\widehat{w})^{2}]
. Since
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w-w=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(w-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
, we bound
\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]\leq\max_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}C^{-1}_{\xi}q]|=|\mathbb{E}[C^{-1}_{\xi}\{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}\widetilde{q}]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}=\argmax_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})q]|.

Here, we use
C_{\xi}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{W}_{1}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{Q}_{1}
. Therefore, if we can bound
|\mathbb{E}[C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\widetilde{q}]|
, the convergence rate is obtained. This is bounded as in the proof of
Theorem

4.1
. Thus, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}-w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\widehat{w}-\widehat{w}\}^{2}]\leq C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},w}.

Convergence rate of q-functions regarding
\|\mathcal{B}\widehat{q}-\widehat{q}\|_{2}

Recall that estimators for q-functions in MIL are defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{q}=\argmin_{q\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{n}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)]|,\,f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,q)=w(s,a)\{r-q(s,a)+\gamma v(s^{\prime})\}

We write this solution to
\widehat{q}
. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{\mathrm{mil}_{2}}(s,a,r,s^{\prime};w,\widehat{q})]=\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})].

What we want to bound is
\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{B}\widehat{q}-\widehat{q}\})^{2}]
. Since
\mathcal{B}q-q=\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
, we bound
\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\}^{2}]\leq C^{-1}_{\xi}\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|=C^{-1}_{\xi}|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}=\argmax_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

Here, we use
C_{\xi}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{2}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{W}_{2}
. Therefore, if we can bound
|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
, the convergence rate is obtained. This is bounded as in the proof of
Theorem

4.1
. Thus, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\mathcal{B}\widehat{q}-\widehat{q}\}^{2}]\leq C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},q}.

Convergence rate of q-functions regarding
\|\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\|_{2}

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}^{2}]\leq\max_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}C^{-1}_{\xi}|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}w(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|=C^{-1}_{\xi}|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|,

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}=\argmax_{w\in\mathbb{W}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}[w\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|.

Here, we use
C_{\xi}(\mathbb{Q}_{2}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathbb{W}_{2}
. Therefore, if we can bound
|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{w}\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})]|
, the convergence rate is obtained. This is bounded as in the proof of
Theorem

4.1
. Thus, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\}^{2}]\leq C^{-1}_{\xi}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{mil},q}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

Statement I:
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{Q}

Recall

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}f(s,a)=\int f(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})P_{S^{\prime}|S,A}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime}).

Thus, for any
q\in\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathcal{B}q
belongs to
\mathbb{Q}
. Noting
\mathcal{B}q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
,
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
also belongs to
\mathbb{Q}
. We write
q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=\{\theta^{*}\}^{\top}\phi
.

Statement II:
\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{Q}

We show there exists
M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
s.t.
\mathcal{T}(\beta^{\top}\phi)=(M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\beta)^{\top}\phi
. This is proved by

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathcal{T}(\beta^{\top}\phi)=\beta^{\top}\{\int\phi(s^{\prime},\pi^{\mathrm{e}})\vartheta(s^{\prime})^{\top}\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime})\}\phi.

Thus, for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle(\gamma\mathcal{T}-I)\{(\theta-\theta^{*})^{\top}\phi\}

\displaystyle=\gamma(\theta-\theta^{*})^{\top}M^{\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\phi-(\theta-\theta^{*})^{\top}\phi

\displaystyle=\{(\gamma M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)(\theta-\theta^{*})\}^{\top}\phi.

This implies
\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}(\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})\subset\mathbb{Q}
. Recall
\mathbb{Q}=\{(s,a)\mapsto\theta^{\top}\phi(s,a);\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
.

Statement III:
\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathbb{Q}

From
Example

12
and (
12
), given
f_{2}=\theta\cdot\phi
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}f_{2}=\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}(\gamma M^{\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}\theta\cdot\phi.

Since
I-\gamma M^{\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is invertible from the assumption
1/\gamma\notin\sigma(M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}})
, for any
q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=(\theta-\theta^{*})\cdot\phi\in\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
, we can take

\displaystyle q^{\prime}=\{\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]^{-1}(\gamma M^{\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}-I)\mathbb{E}[\phi\phi^{\top}]\}^{-1}(\theta-\theta^{*})\cdot\phi

s.t.
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}q^{\prime}=q-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}

This implies
\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathbb{Q}

Statement IV: Tabular case

We use a matrix formulation following
Example

11
. In a tabular case,
M_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=P_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
. The spectrum of
P_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
is less than or equal to
1
. Thus,
\mathbb{Q}-q_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\subset\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\mathbb{Q}
holds from the statement III. Besides,
\mathbb{Q}=\{\theta^{\top}\phi;\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
from the statement II.

∎

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

From Lemma
B.1
, recall

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f(s,a)=\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\int f(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})P_{S,A|S^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}|s)\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}).

First, by using the assumption
P_{S,A|S^{\prime}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\cdot)=\psi(\cdot)\cdot\vartheta(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}f
belongs to

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}=\{(s,a)\mapsto\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s,a)\times(\psi(s)\cdot\beta);\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}.

Especially,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\{\beta^{\top}\eta\psi\}=(M^{\prime}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\beta)^{\top}\eta\psi
holds by taking
M^{\prime\top}_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
s.t.

\displaystyle\int\eta_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\psi(s^{\prime})\vartheta^{\top}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\mu(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}).

Statement I :
w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}\in\mathbb{W}

Noting
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w=\gamma\mathcal{T}^{\prime}w+(1-\gamma)\frac{d_{0}(s)\pi^{\mathrm{e}}(a|s)}{\pi^{\mathrm{b}}(a|s)p_{S}(s)}
and the assumption
d_{0}(s)/p_{S}(s)
belongs to
\{\psi(s)\cdot\beta);\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
, for any
w
,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w
belongs to
\mathbb{W}
. Since
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}=w_{\pi^{\mathrm{e}}}
,
